The link is Ghost's to manage, so `preserveDecisionLinks` composes the caller's reference with the links the row already holds (`extendDecisionRefs`): the caller's own value is kept WHOLE beside the link, the way a consolidation successor carries `runbook.md,decision:<id>`, and the composed value is held to `MaxSourceRefLen` — a reference that does not fit beside the links is REFUSED rather than shortened, because a truncated path is a different path and the link is never traded for the reference (the same rule `boundedSourceRef` applies to an over-long reference on its own). The stored value is read in the transaction that writes the row, so the links composed are the ones the row holds at the moment the write lands, and both writers that store a reference on an EXISTING row ask it: `UpdateMemoryWithOptions` (the edit tool's path) and `UpsertWithOptions`' fold branch (a save that folds onto a companion writes its reference onto the row it folded into, so the same hole was open one door over). Pinned by `TestASourceRefEditKeepsTheDecisionLinkOnTheCompanion`, `TestASupersedeAfterASourceRefEditStillRetiresTheCompanion`, `TestASourceRefEditThatDoesNotFitBesideTheLinkIsRefused`, `TestASaveThatFoldsOntoTheCompanionKeepsTheDecisionLink` and `TestAMemoryUpdateKeepsTheDecisionLinkOnTheCompanion`. **A consolidation that FOLDS TWO decision companions into one successor makes that successor stand for BOTH decisions (#1206):** `inheritedClaims` EXTENDS the reference the ordinary rules chose (the emission's own, else the first source's) with every distinct link its sources carry (`extendDecisionRefs`) rather than keeping the first or replacing the value — replacing discarded the emission's stated reference, and keeping one value dropped a lone link behind a non-decision first source (#1220) — and holds the result to `MaxSourceRefLen` (`boundedDecisionRefs`, since a composed value over it would not re-import): a base that no longer fits beside the links gives way to them, and a merge whose links ALONE do not fit is dropped by `ReplaceNonManual` before anything is written (`decisionLinksOverflowTx`, which reads the rows' `source_ref` and then asks the exported pure `DecisionLinksOverflow` — the one copy of the rule, which the reflect accounting calls over the input rows so a dry run lists the same merge as refused), its sources kept live as a refused claim's are, rather than truncating the list or failing the pass; THAT DROP IS REPORTED UNDER ITS OWN REASON AND DOES NOT HOLD THE PROJECT OPEN (#1228): the refusals a replace makes are counted by reason in `memory.EmissionDrops` (`Changed` = the corpus guard refused a claim on a row that moved, `Overflow` = this drop, with the link count beside it, `Scope` = a merge whose sources' scopes disagree, permanent like `Overflow` and likewise not holding the project open, #1492), because the two have OPPOSITE consequences for the caller — a `Changed` row is still live and still needs merging, so no skip fingerprint may be recorded over it, while an overflowing merge cannot be written by any later run either, so recording the fingerprint is what stops `--skip-unchanged` re-proposing and dropping the same merge on every round forever. The rows an overflow drop keeps alive are therefore NOT reported in `preserved` (`keepEmissionClaims` returns the ids it marked so the replace can tell a keep that only the overflow made): a survivor is a row a later run can still do something about, and `cmd/ghost reflect` reads a non-empty `preserved` as exactly that. It also prints the overflow as its own line — how many decision links, and the `MaxSourceRefLen` bound — rather than folding it into "an emission claimed a memory that changed or was deleted", which names a row that did not move and a repair no later run can perform. and the lookup matches a link inside that joined list as a TOKEN (`sourceRefNamesDecision`, with `decisionRefsInSourceRef` counting them) rather than by equality, so a successor carrying `decision:<a>,decision:<c>` is found for either. The separator is a comma, and a comma cannot occur inside a link on ANY writer: `RecordDecision` mints hex ids, and `CheckImportedDecision` refuses the separator in an imported decision id (the one writer that takes an id verbatim) — an id holding it would split its own link into tokens that name no decision, so the supersede would find no companion and answer clean. While another decision it links is not superseded (`linksAnotherUnsupersededDecisionTx`; a link to a decision the store does not hold counts as not superseded) it is no decision's clean companion, so a live one is returned in `DecisionRetirement.Shared` — its own channel, NOT `Ambiguous`, whose answer allows deleting a claimant that holds nothing else, which for a shared row would destroy the only memory of a live decision — and never retired; the rows carrying the decision's link alone are judged as if the shared rows were absent, so a sole single-link companion beside a shared successor is still retired and the `decision_log` preference never picks a shared row; once every OTHER decision it links is superseded it states nothing live and is judged as this decision's own companion, so the last supersede retires it instead of reporting it as shared forever — retiring it when the first is superseded would withhold whatever the second still states, and a single-value link would let superseding the second answer clean while its reversed text stayed live. A single link keeps the first-source-wins rule unchanged. **`DistinctiveTerms` (#973) is the keyword terms a CALLER may judge a match by, and it is `ftsQueryTerms`'s own extraction** rather than a second tokenizer: stopwords dropped, repeats collapsed, identifier-shaped terms (and a dotted file-name-shaped term) first, bounded at 8 KiB of input and 4,096 terms before selection so the sanitizer's truncation warning (a stderr line) can never fire on a path that must stay silent. **A QUERY TERM SURVIVES THE EDGE TRIM IN WHATEVER SCRIPT IT IS WRITTEN (#1701).** `ftsQueryTerms` trims a word's edges to what the index's own tokenizer reads as part of a word, and the kept set is a letter, a digit and a mark of ANY script (`ftsEdgeRuneTrimmed`), not the ASCII letters and digits it was: a word in a script the ASCII set does not name trimmed to nothing, so the query reached the index as an EMPTY term list — the current leg handed FTS5 a MATCH with no term in it, and the historical leg ran no keyword pass at all — and a memory written in that script could not be found by a query in it, on EITHER path (the `as_of` path has no vector leg to reach it by). The widening changes WHICH terms survive the trim and nothing else: a surviving term is quoted and escaped exactly as an ASCII term is (an interior quote doubled, a trailing `*` outside the quotes), the term budget is the budget (`ftsSearchWordLimit`, ten), and the identifier ranking and the stopword rule are untouched — a word in another script is a CONTENT word, because the shapes the identifier tier knows are code-domain English ones. An ASCII query therefore still produces the byte-identical MATCH it always did. **THE LIMIT IS THE TOKENIZER'S, NOT THE TRIM'S:** `memories_fts` uses `tokenize='porter unicode61'`, which reads a run of letters with no space between them as ONE token, so a CJK query matches a whole run (or a prefix of one, with a trailing `*`) and never a word inside it — real CJK segmentation needs a trigram or an ICU tokenizer, which is a schema change and a separate decision. Every script whose words are written with spaces (Cyrillic, Arabic, Greek, Hebrew, Devanagari) matches word for word, exactly as an ASCII word does. An ACCENTED Latin word is the one case the two paths read differently, and the difference is the tokenizer's: FTS5 folds diacritics (so a query for `café` also reaches a row written `cafe`), while the historical matcher matches the spelling as written — the asymmetry `tokenizeForMatch` states, and the one the ASCII trim used to hide by dropping every accented edge. Pinned by `TestCandidatesMatchesANonLatinQuery`, `TestCandidatesMatchesANonLatinPrefixQuery`, `TestCandidatesAsOfMatchesANonLatinQuery`, `TestCandidatesAsOfMatchesANonLatinPrefixQuery` and `TestSanitizeFTSKeepsNonLatinWords` in `internal/memory`. **A TASK'S `completed_at` FOLLOWS ITS STATUS AND ONLY ITS STATUS (#1040):** `Store.UpdateTask` (behind `ghost_task_update`) stamps `completed_at` on the transition INTO `done` and clears it (SQL NULL) on any transition out, matching `Store.CompleteTask`; an already-`done` task edited again — the status repeated or left nil — keeps the stamp it got when it BECAME done rather than being re-stamped, because the column records completion and not the last write. The returned `Task` carries the post-merge value, so a caller needs no second read to see it. `completed_at` already round-trips through the portable export. **A PROJECT MERGE CARRIES THE OUTGOING PROJECT'S LEARNED CONTEXT OVER (#1045):** `mergeGhostStateTx` — one helper, called by both `mergeProjectTx` copies and by the merge inside `migrateV16`, so the two cannot drift — treats `learned_context`, `reflection_summary`, `reflect_input_sig` and `last_reflection_at` as ONE unit, because `UpdateLearnedContext` writes all four from a single reflection pass and a merged row that took one project's learned context with the other's summary, signature and timestamp would describe a reflection that never happened. The unit is taken whole from the outgoing project when the survivor has no reflection at all (no timestamp AND empty learned context) or when both projects have a timestamped reflection and the outgoing one is newer; otherwise the survivor keeps its whole unit, including a survivor that holds learned context but no timestamp (it is the project the merge is into, and a missing timestamp is not evidence the outgoing reflection is newer). `interaction_count` is not part of the unit — it is a monotonic counter, so the larger value is kept. The survivor row is `INSERT OR IGNORE`d before the UPDATE, so a merge into a project that never reflected receives the outgoing context rather than matching no UPDATE row and losing it. **A MANIFEST MUST BE A REGULAR FILE, AND A SYMLINK TO ONE IS FOLLOWED (#1127):** `ReadBackupManifest` `stat`s the resolved target BEFORE opening it and refuses a FIFO, device, socket or directory (directly or through a link) as present-but-unreadable without opening it, because opening a FIFO blocks until a writer appears and would hang `ghost backup verify`. **ABSENCE IS DECIDED BY THE `Lstat`, NOT BY THE READ'S ERROR (#1092):** `VerifyBackup` takes `ManifestPresent` from an `Lstat` and treats `ReadBackupManifest`'s `fs.ErrNotExist` as a missing manifest ONLY when that `Lstat` also found nothing. A dangling symlink at the manifest path makes the read report ENOENT exactly as a genuinely absent file does, so the two are indistinguishable from the error alone and the flag is the only thing that separates them; gating on it keeps a present-but-unreadable sidecar a refusal carrying a real `ManifestErr`, instead of the report printing `present but could not be read: <nil>` under a `checked` verdict and a zero exit that a backup script reads as nothing failed. **A MANIFEST'S `sha256` IS A DIGEST OR THE MANIFEST IS UNREADABLE (#1187):** the sidecar arrives with the copy being judged and is untrusted input, so `ReadBackupManifest` refuses a `sha256` that is not exactly 64 lowercase hex digits as present-but-unreadable, naming the value through `RefusalArg`, and `checkHash` refuses to print one it has not validated; printed raw, `ESC [1F` rewrote `refusing` as `verified` on screen. `memory.VerifyBackup` reports one row per check whether it ran or not: a check that could not run is `skipped` and names the read that failed, never dropped from the table, and the schema version and the row counts are read separately, so one failing read does not silence the other — a report that lost those two rows would read like a build that only has two checks, and an operator deciding whether to keep a damaged copy needs to know which questions were asked THE PASSIVE THRESHOLD SCAN'S WINDOW ORDER ENDS IN id ASC, AND ITS TIE ORDER DOES THE SAME: the scan reads rows by importance DESC, then the policy's secondary key (created_at for decay, updated_at for pinned_importance_updated), then id. The id term is load-bearing: it is the window's final tie-break, and the stop is now NON-STRICT (passiveRankKeyStopsAt fires when a row's importance is <= the window's k-th key), so without id a row whose importance AND timestamp tie the window's edge arrives in rowid order and a smaller-id row that belongs inside can arrive after the stop. Two composite indexes carry the order: idx_memories_project_imp on (project_id, importance DESC, created_at DESC, id) and idx_memories_project_imp_updated on (project_id, importance DESC, updated_at DESC, id). The widened definitions and the companion index are installed by migrateV27, which drops and recreates the first because an existing narrower index under the same name is not widened by CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS. A read of `memory_links` whose caller applies, reports or truncates the rows in the order they arrive names that order: `LinksByRelationSource` and `LinksInto` end in `created_at, rowid` (`created_at` has one-second resolution and `rowid` is the insertion order), because an unordered join returns whatever the plan visits first and an index on `memories` changes the plan (`TestLinksByRelationSourceReturnsEdgesInInsertionOrder`). Readers that fold the rows into a set or a map are exempt. THE FULL-TEXT INDEX IS BUCKETED BY PROJECT, AND EVERY PROJECT-SCOPED MATCH READS ITS BUCKETS ALONE (schema v28, #1563). An FTS row's rowid is the project's ordinal in `memories_fts_projects` times 2^40 plus the memory's rowid, so one project's rows are one rowid range; `ftsMatchSource` (`internal/memory/fts_bucket.go`) bounds the FTS5 scan to that range INSIDE the virtual table (`rowid >= ord*2^40 AND rowid < (ord+1)*2^40`, shown as `M1><` in the plan), and a project-plus-`_global` read is the `UNION ALL` of two bounded scans. The statements that use it are both save-time duplicate lookups (`dedupLookupStatement`, inside `UpsertWithOptions`' `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`), the keyword leg (`ftsSearchStatement`, behind `SearchFTS` and the query path) and `keywordValidityExcluded`'s count; `SearchFTSAll` is the one unbounded scan. Before it, FTS5 enumerated, ranked, joined and sorted every project's matches before the project filter and the `LIMIT` applied, so a save's write-lock hold grew with OTHER projects' rows (`TestFTSStatementsBoundTheScanToTheProject` pins every statement's plan). The index's columns and tokens are unchanged, so bm25 is the value it always was and results are the same rows in the same order (`TestBucketedFTSMatchesTheStoreWideScan`; the statements end their ORDER BY in `m.rowid`, which is the order the store-wide scan delivered tied rows in, and is what keeps a tie between a project row and a `_global` row in place across the two arms). KNOWN LIMIT: bm25's statistics are store-wide by definition — each phrase's document frequency is counted over the whole index once per ranked statement (FTS5's `xQueryPhrase`) — so a ranked lookup still pays a per-term pass over the store's doclists; only per-project statistics would remove it, and those change the ranking. The triggers keep the bucket in sync: `memories_ai` creates a project's ordinal before its first row is indexed, `memories_au` fires on a content change OR a project change (a promotion to `_global` moves the row's bucket, so its FTS rowid changes), and `memories_ad` and `memories_au` delete the ordinal once its project's last memory has left, so `memories_fts_projects` holds exactly the project ids `memories` holds and an ordinal is never reassigned while an FTS row carries it; a project that comes back gets a new one. Every trigger statement that writes the ordinal table is a conflict-free `INSERT ... WHERE NOT EXISTS`, never `OR IGNORE`: an outer `INSERT OR REPLACE INTO memories` would turn a trigger's `OR IGNORE` into a REPLACE that re-numbers the ordinal under the rows still carrying it. The index's external content is the view `memories_fts_source` (the purge path's and the migration's `'rebuild'` read it), and the view is NOT in initSQL (`memoriesFTSSourceSQL`): initSQL runs before a lagging store is migrated, and a view naming `memories` makes the legacy table rebuilds' `ALTER TABLE ... RENAME TO memories` fail, so it is created by `ensurePostMigrationIndexes` and by `migrateV28`. A future step that rebuilds `memories` that way must drop the view first. `migrateV28` drops the old triggers, index and view, renumbers the ordinals from `memories` in `project_id` order, recreates them and rebuilds the index; every object is dropped before it is created, so the step is idempotent (`TestOpenDBMigratesAV27StoreToTheBucketedIndex` compares a v27 store's answers before and after, the schema with a fresh store's, and a second run). A memory's rowid must stay below 2^40; nothing in Ghost writes an explicit one. THE EMBEDDING IDENTITY IS READ WITHOUT THE STORE'S MUTEX ON EVERY PATH — `embeddingIdentity` is an atomic and every reader loads it, the search seams and the two readers that filter by model included — because it is process configuration, set once at startup and not changed while the server serves: taking the mutex for it made a reader queue behind an in-flight write for a value that cannot change, and each of those readers still takes the mutex for its own read, so no reader waits less than it did before (#1738).


**TWO BOUNDED READS PER PASS — ONE SNAPSHOT, TAKEN BY ONE DEFERRED READ TRANSACTION**, read-only, and both consumers FAIL OPEN on an unreadable map (the annotation is an addition to a judgement that works without it; failing the pass would make the audit a dependency of maintenance rather than an input to it). The two reads are the project's negative verdicts and then the content of exactly the memories those verdicts name — **ONE ROW PER MEMORY**, because a single `JOIN`ed statement would project a full copy of `memories.content` (the largest field in the table) once per VERDICT row, where `retrieval_audit` is capped at 50 000 rows and `memories.content` is unbounded — and the transaction is what makes the pair ONE SNAPSHOT, so a verdict another process deleted between the reads is never counted. `UsefulnessEvidence.Line` renders ONE fixed format from counts and a session id, and the id is rendered through **memory.SafeToken — an ALLOWLIST, not a denylist**, which is the same function `assemble.Token` delegates to, so there is one implementation of the rule rather than one per reader. The id is **BOUNDED TO 64 RUNES FIRST and quoted second**, and the order is load-bearing: bounding second would cut an ESCAPE rather than a rune and return a malformed literal. A value is written **bare when every rune is allowlisted** — ASCII letters, digits and the seven separators `._-:/@+`, which is every id Ghost mints (32 hex characters) and every scope name — and otherwise **the WHOLE id becomes one ASCII-quoted Go string**. It is an allowlist because a denylist can only list what somebody thought of: the renderer this replaced was one, and it passed U+2028 and U+0085 (LINE TERMINATORS to JavaScript and to several newline-splitting readers), a bidi override that reverses how the rest of a line displays, and a zero-width space that no reader can see. The set is pinned EXACTLY by `TestTheBareTokenAllowlistIsExactlyTheDocumentedSet`, exhaustively over every code point, because a table of hostile examples passes whenever the set is widened by a character nobody listed — and by `TestTheBareTokenAllowlistHoldsNothingInvisible`, which names the exclusions that are printable and therefore hard to notice by eye. Note the ceiling the rune bound does NOT give: under ASCII-only quoting a BMP rune escapes to six characters and an ASTRAL one to ten, so 64 escaping runes render 642 bytes and the line 703 — measured, against 125 for a bare id, where the renderer it replaced bounded the OUTPUT at about 64. It is still a constant, which is what "without limit" has to mean. The bound exists because reflect lists one memory per line inside «...» and a stored id carrying `\n- id:E1 …` would forge a record the model then emits an operation against. A memory with NO audit rows renders NOTHING, not an empty bracket: `TestAMemoryWithNoAuditRowsProducesThePromptItProducedBefore` and its resolve twin compare the whole prompt byte for byte, so an unaudited project is unaffected by this and cannot be blamed for a verdict that moved. resolve puts the line INSIDE the note's own content, so it lands in the «...» the classifier's `quoteData` renders and is read under the rubric's own data rule; and `reassess` deliberately reads none (its evidence points toward staying resolved, the opposite of a repair). **THE EVIDENCE IS READ ABOVE THE CACHE GATE, AND THE STAMP COVERS IT** (#880): `resolve.Run` reads the map ONCE per pass whenever any candidate reaches the cache gate, and the gate matches `keptHashes[m.ID] == KeepStamp(m.Content, evidence[m.ID])` — `memory.ContentHash(content)` BYTE FOR BYTE when the audit has no negative verdict about that content, and the content hash combined with the two negative counts plus the latest negative verdict's stamp when it does. So a pre-#880 hash stored for un-doubted content is still a cache hit and no `keepCacheHashVersion` bump was needed, while a contradiction recorded AFTER the KEEP does not match: the memory goes back in the pending set, is re-asked ONCE with the line attached, and the KEEP that comes back is stamped with that same evidence, so the next pass matches and skips until the audit records another verdict, which moves the stamp once more. **A negative verdict RE-ASKS; it never RESCUES.** It reaches the classifier as data and the classifier decides, exactly as it decides everything else — never `resolve` on its own, and never a demotion. The bound is what makes the cost arguable: a pass with nothing at the gate (nothing prefiltered, or nothing the veto did not settle) pays no read at all, and the only NEW payer is the converged pass, which pays ONE bounded read and still zero classifier calls (measured by `TestMeasuredCostOfTheConvergedPassEvidenceRead`). Positive verdicts cannot move a stamp — `used` and `ignored` are filtered in SQL — so a memory that is merely retrieved often can never re-ask itself, which is the #284 loop closed at the reader rather than at this comparison. The content key itself is unchanged: a retag or a re-weight still re-asks nothing, because neither is evidence about the content — an audit verdict arrives independently of the content, which is why it rides in the stamp instead of replacing the hash. The repair pass compares the BARE content hash and reads no evidence (#880 re-examined that exemption and kept it): it cannot construct a stamp covering evidence it never read, so a stamp carrying evidence costs one re-ask per repair run over that row, erring toward asking; the ordinary pass then re-stamps it and both are quiet in between. The cache gate in `internal/resolve/resolve.go` says the same where a reader of the gate will meet it. `InputSignature` is deliberately unchanged, so `--skip-unchanged` still skips a corpus that has merely been audited — mirroring the evidence would stop the gate skipping on any store that records verdicts, and the audit grows on most sessions.
**A DIAGNOSTIC READ IS TAKEN ON THE READ HANDLE, IN ONE READ TRANSACTION, AND TAKES NONE OF THE STORE'S MUTEX (#1738).** A write holds `s.mu` for its whole transaction, and the primary handle is pinned to one connection, so a reader that goes through the primary handle waits for the write to commit: `ghost_health` answered `list projects: context canceled` and no report at all while a project delete was in flight, and the store-version line it never reached was the first thing the report lost. `Store.BeginDiagnosticRead` opens ONE read transaction on the read-only handle (`memory.OpenReadDB`, whose DSN carries no `_txlock`, so the transaction is a plain deferred `BEGIN`) and hands back a VIEW of the store bound to it: the readers a report is made of read through `queryDB`, so their statements come from that one transaction and describe ONE committed state, and the view takes none of the parent store's mutex, so the report answers while the write it describes is still in flight. The view carries the store's vector identity across, because coverage counted against another identity would report vectors this process cannot search with — and the identity is read WITHOUT the mutex, since it is process configuration set once at startup (`embeddingIdentity` is an atomic for that reason, and every reader loads it). A store with NO read handle (an in-memory store, a bench store, a store built without `NewStoreWithRead`) has no diagnostic read: `BeginDiagnosticRead` returns none and the caller falls back to the store's own reads, which is what those stores have always used and the right answer for a store with no second process to be blocked by. Guarded by `TestTheDiagnosticReadAnswersWhileAWriteIsInFlight` and `TestAStoreWithNoReadHandleHasNoDiagnosticRead`.

- `internal/assemble/` — context assembly: `assemble.Run` is the one entry point (one `[]stage` in `pipeline.go`), owning selection, validity, category/scope predicates before window closure, provenance (pinned at 1.0), budget closure, the outcome, the `response_fit` post-pass, the always-recorded trace and the shared `Item.Line()` renderer. **THE FIT RULE IS ONE IMPLEMENTATION (`assemble.FitItems`, #1706).** The response-fit post-pass is that function, and so is the session-start block's historical read (`ghost context --as-of`), whose rows are not the assembler's but which must be fitted by the same rule: the lowest-ranked row goes first, an UNPINNED one before any pinned one (`Budget.KeepPinned`), the block is re-measured after every cut, and a framing alone over the caller's ceiling (`Budget.FramingCeiling`) keeps its rows rather than being cut for nothing, the question asked once against the framing with EVERY row cut. A caller supplies its own measure (the size of its own render), its own all-cut framing (the size with every row cut, which is what the ceiling is decided against) and its own cut; the rule owns the order, the stop conditions and the once-only ceiling question. Two copies of a fit are two fits, and the second one is the one nobody updates. A live `contradicts` pair whose scopes do not conflict is SEPARATED by stage 5 (#925): stage 5 walks the rows that have a live edge in keep-priority order and withholds any row that contradicts one it already kept. The winner is chosen by a documented tie-break — pinned beats unpinned, a later `verified_at` beats an earlier or absent one, a later `updated_at` (falling back to `created_at`) beats an earlier one, and an exact tie falls to the rank the window already holds; relevance rank decides nothing before the last key. The withheld rows are dropped at `stageConflicts` with reason `contradiction_separated` and `Decision.Against` naming the kept row or rows they directly contradict, the kept line names the rows it directly contradicts that were withheld through `conflicts_with=` + the other row's `Token` (a field on the same line, never a new line), bounded at `maxRenderedConflictPartners` (8) named partners in rank order with any remainder reported as a trailing `(+N more)` count, so a dense graph cannot spend one line's share of the response budget; the answer note and the explain reason are derived from the recorded pairs against the rows the answer finally holds, so a pair whose winner the budget later cut names nothing. `pipeline.markConflicts` is the one place the marker is decided; it runs on every pass of `fitResponse` so the `response_fit` drop of a winner clears it, rebuilds from the edges stage 5 recorded (`conflictPartners`) rather than from the separations, and skips an edge whose endpoints' scopes conflict (`memory.ScopesConflict`); `mcpinit` carries `Item.ConflictsWith` through `sessionMemory` rather than recomputing it. Depends on one method, `Candidates`; `internal/memory` never imports it. `ScopeLabel` is the scope label `Item.Line()` embeds and the session-start block prints, so no surface re-derives it; `ScopeContradicts` is the named negation of `memory.ScopeMatches` rather than a second copy of the rule. `Result.Leaks()` and `Trace.TrimmedByBudget()` are the ONLY way a consumer learns which rows it should not have carried and what the budget cut, and both READ the verdicts the stages recorded (`Trace.Signals[id]`: `ValidityState`, `ScopeMatched`, `ProjectMatch`, plus the row's `resolved_at`) rather than re-deciding them — a second classifier anywhere, and the benchmark is the surface that wants one, is a second implementation of the same rules free to drift until a leak is reported as clean by the only component whose job is to say otherwise. `LeakArms` is the fixed report order with zeros included, and a consumer meeting an arm it has no row for refuses rather than counting that row clean. The session-start block is a caller of `Run` (`loadSessionPassive`, `internal/mcpinit/session_passive.go`), and it is the reason the golden block is byte-identical for every branch its fixture exercises: the block's rows are chosen by ONE `assemble.Run` with two bucket policies, where before they were chosen by two private queries with two private over-fetches, two private two-pass selections and two private near-duplicate passes, so the assembler's stages ran over rows a second implementation of the same rules had already picked. A `SourceSessionStart` with an empty `Query` is a PASSIVE request, and `Store.Candidates` serves it from `CandidateRequest.Passive`; a source alone is not what makes it passive, which is why the empty `Query` is load-bearing rather than decorative. The golden's byte-identity is scoped rather than absolute, and the exception is the `_global` bucket's SUPERSEDE demotion: `loadGlobalMemories` ran only the near-duplicate lookup over global rows, while the assembler's passive demotion runs `SupersedePenalties` for every bucket with no field to decline it, so a `supersedes` edge between two `_global` rows now pushes the replaced row out of a bucket capped at 8 — a membership change, not a reorder. It is kept, because that bucket already DROPS its near-duplicate losers on the stated ground that a superseded preference is not worth one of eight cross-project slots, and an explicit edge is a stronger signal than a cosine neighbour. `TestTheGlobalBucketNowDemotesSupersededRows` states it, and the golden cannot: its one `supersedes` edge joins two project rows. PASSIVE is keyed on the ABSENCE OF A QUERY (`assemble.Passive`), not on `Source`, because the consequences follow from the absence: no leg ran, so no FTS rank and no cosine exist to compare, a passive block is NEVER `weak` (it is `answerable`/`not_applicable` — `weak` would be a relevance verdict about a question the surface was never asked), and an empty passive window is `no_memories` rather than `no_candidates` because the passive path never counts the corpus; that verdict reaches the trace and the retrieval record and the session-start block does not print it (its only verdict-derived text is `assemble.EmptyNote`, which is empty for `no_memories`). Both wordings are refusals in the other direction and each is a different lie: `no matching memories found` claims a census that was never taken, and `no sufficiently trustworthy memory found` claims rows were found and rejected when the window came back empty. A window that came back NON-empty and was then emptied by a stage IS an exclusion and says so in the shared wording; and that exclusion outranks the leg reasons on BOTH paths. When `CandidateSet.ValidityExcluded > 0` — the count a leg takes over the rows its own SQL window predicate withheld before its `LIMIT` — the reason is `all_invalid`, named AHEAD of `vector_backend_unavailable` and `retrieval_failed`, because rows were found and withheld is a fact about the STORE while a leg that never ran or broke is a fact about the MACHINE: naming the leg (`ghost_memory_search` always asks for a hybrid search, which on a machine with no embedder leaves the vector leg applicable and never attempted) sent the caller after an embedder that was never the reason there was no answer, and discarded the retired row entirely. The leg's state is not lost — it stays on the machine line (`legs=fts:ok,vector:not_run`) — and ONE check serves the passive and query paths, so the precedence cannot drift between them. TWO passive requests are REFUSED rather than served, both because the seam cannot honour what they ask and would answer a confident false negative instead. One: a slice's bucket must be the requested project or `_global` (`validatePassiveBudget`). The bucket IS the project predicate — the store binds `SlicePolicy.Bucket` as the `WHERE` clause and never consults `Mode` — so a mismatched bucket would READ AND INJECT a project the request never named, and stage 3 only RECORDS that as `Signals[id].ProjectMatch=false`, which nothing refuses; the same decision `sliceBuckets` makes for a repeated bucket, one named bucket per slice, and it has to be a bucket this request is about. Two: a passive request cannot carry a `Category` or `Retention` filter. `passiveKeyScanSQL` binds no category and no tier in its SQL at all, while the query path WIDENS the window when one is set (a category, a tier or a scope) (`predicateFetchWiden`, which is what lets a matching row below the cut stay reachable) precisely so a matching row below the cut stays reachable — and the passive branch of `retrievalWindow` returns before that widening, because the window there is the policies' own over-fetches. So the filter never applied at all: stage 3 drops every non-matching row out of a window nothing widened, and the caller gets `all_out_of_category` while the store holds the rows it asked for just below the cut. That is the damage, and it is why this is a refusal and not a widened fetch: a window is a bound, and an over-reported one costs a field's honesty, but a filter decides MEMBERSHIP, and a caller told its category is absent from the project will not widen anything. `abstain_cosine` therefore reports `not_applied` for a passive block rather than `off`: `off` is a configuration a reader can change, and the session-start surface has no vector arm to configure, whatever `context.abstain_cosine` is set to. The budget is what carries the bucket policies — `Slice` gains `OverFetch`/`Order`/`TwoPass`/`BehaviorFloor`/`BehaviorCategories`/`CategoryWeights`/`CategoryCaps`/`DemotionThreshold`/`DemoteOnlyWhenOverCap` — because a passive retrieval has no query to shape it, and a passive slice stating neither an over-fetch nor a cap is REFUSED at both seams: passive runs at every session start, and an unbounded fetch is a store scan. `Slice.MaxItems`/`MaxBytes` still bound membership, and `Budget.MaxItems`/`MaxBytes` stay 0 for both passive surfaces because `Budget.MaxBytes` bounds the complete rendered RESPONSE, which is the search framing a differently-framed surface must not spend (see `Result`). A `SourceProjectCtx` with an empty `Query` is the same PASSIVE shape, and it backs FOUR agent-facing surfaces: `ghost_project_context`, the `ghost://project/{id}/context` resource and the `recall_project` prompt share ONE budget statement (`projectContextBudget`), so the tool's caller-supplied `limit` and the resource's two fixed read caps — a 20-row project window, and a 15-row `_global` second read the resource's section adds to that window's own `_global` half — cannot drift apart, and the tool's block is byte-identical through the move — the fixture seeds no validity column, so the one behaviour the move changes (a closed or not-yet-opened window is DROPPED rather than rendered with an `expired` marker, because stage 2 runs unconditionally) is pinned by `TestTheProjectContextDropsRowsWhoseWindowHasClosed` and NOT by the goldens, which would have to be re-recorded to hold it and a re-recorded baseline is a diff record rather than a proof. Its bucket is the one that sets `IncludeGlobal`, so the assembler's per-slice cap is keyed on `Candidate.FetchedBy` — the POLICY that admitted the row — rather than on the row's own project (`capBucket`), and a request that both mixes `_global` into one bucket and fetches it in another is REFUSED (`validatePassiveBudget`): the overlap is invisible to `sliceBuckets` because the names differ, every global row would be admitted twice, and neither slice's cap would describe the block. The check resolves the flag across the whole budget BEFORE the loop, because a version that sets it as it walks refuses one slice order and serves the other — a refusal a caller talks past by sorting. `Request.Record` is a per-REQUEST `RecordSink` (no package global, so two Runs cannot record into each other's store, and nil — the bench and every test here — records nothing) that receives ONE `memory.RetrievalRecord` per SUCCESSFUL Run, and the projection in `record.go` is a THIRD reader of the trace's verdicts beside `Leaks()` and `TrimmedByBudget()`: kept rows come from `Result.Items` and dropped rows from `Trace.Decisions`, and nothing is re-derived. It is set by THREE surfaces and no others, all three through the same seam: `ghost_memory_search` in its handler, `assembleProjectContext` for the tool, the resource and the prompt, and `loadSessionPassive` for the session-start block (#850 — before it, only the search recorded, so every per-source denominator in the audit described searches). Its placement is the whole correctness of the record — `emit` runs after `fitResponse` and only when it returned no error, because the post-pass drops rows from the bottom of the ranking and records each as a `response_fit` decision (a record written earlier would name a row the caller never received as KEPT, which is the false "used" verdict that flatters Ghost), and because a failed retrieval, a refused request and an unfittable envelope all return an error instead of a Result, and a record for any of them would put a denominator in the audit for calls that returned no memories at all. THE POST-PASS DECIDES ONCE WHETHER CUTTING ROWS CAN HELP, FROM A FRAMING MEASURE THAT DOES NOT DEPEND ON THE CUTS (#987): with `Budget.Measure` and a `Budget.FramingCeiling`, the question is asked on the FIRST over-cap iteration and never re-asked, and it is asked against the framing as it stands with every row cut (`allCutTrace`, a COPY of the trace carrying one `response_fit` decision per row, passed to `Measure` with an empty item slice — the longest the caller's count line can ever be). A caller's framing is not independent of the cuts it makes, so a check re-read each iteration read a different question each time, and the order of the readings was the bug: a framing just under the ceiling passed, a row was cut, the clause that cut earned pushed the framing over the ceiling, and the next iteration stopped with the block over its cap and rows recorded as cut for nothing. The hypothetical cut trace is a COPY and never reaches the run's own trace or record; `Budget.Measure` must therefore read a trace holding MORE decisions than the run made as "every row was cut for size" and nothing more. **THE SEARCH ENVELOPE NEVER CUTS ITS LAST ROW (#1483)**: with no `Budget.Measure`, an answer still over `Budget.MaxBytes` with one row left keeps that row whole and carries `assemble.FirstKeptNotice` (the sentence `capResponseParts` shares) in place of the cut notice, which says the row alone is larger than the budget only when its own line is (a content-cap memory whose line breaks fold to `⏎` is one) and otherwise that the row fits but not with the framing around it, so a `response_fit` `all_over_budget` is reached only through a `Budget.Measure` caller, whose fit still cuts down to none (`TestAQueryAnswerKeepsItsOnlyRowWholeOverTheBudget`, `TestAQueryAnswerWhoseFramingIsTheExcessDoesNotClaimTheRowIsTooLarge`, `TestMemorySearchKeepsAMemoryLargerThanTheBudget`). `Request.SuppressRecordWhenLegsFailed` covers the one case `Run` cannot see from inside: a result with a FAILED leg and nothing admitted is a valid `Result` that the MCP handler then CONVERTS into a retryable error, so an agent never reads "nothing matched" as a fact. A row the retrieval window CUT is in neither `Result.Items` nor `Trace.Decisions` and is deliberately NOT recorded: it was never judged, and a verdict for it would claim a decision nobody made. `queryHash` asks the SINK for the store's keyed digest — an HMAC under the per-install key, not a hash of the question — and is EMPTY for an empty query rather than the digest of nothing, because a constant in a digest column reads as a fingerprint and a passive session start is exactly the call that has no question. The one log line `emit` writes when the record cannot be written (a dropped row — the record is a measurement of a call that already happened, so the failure is logged and the search is never failed) renders its `project_id` through `memory.RefusalArg`, NOT as a bare struct field (#1464, the same class as #1327's cooldown skip line): the project id is a STORED value, not this call's own argument, so nothing upstream judged it — `CheckImportedProjectID` refuses a control character but never asks the token guard, and `ghost import` writes an artifact's project id verbatim — so an ordinary id is `%q`-quoted (byte-identical for a reader) and a token-shaped one is withheld behind a placeholder naming the field and the format; `source` and `outcome` stay bare because both are this build's own enum values chosen from a closed set, and a stored id never reaches either. `TestAFailedRecordLogWithholdsATokenShapedProjectID` asserts both halves from one log line: the token-shaped id absent, and an ordinary id still present so the line can name the project whose record was dropped. Any agent-visible rendering of a stored id goes through `Token`, a name or path through `Label`, and free text through `quoteData` inside «…» under the `dataDelimiterNote` line; a new listing tool or resource must use them. `Token` renders a KEY and quotes a space, so `Label` exists for the values that are normally full of them, and it keeps ordinary text byte-identical because that is the point of it. `Label`'s escape test is the whole line-ending class rather than an approximation of it (#1363): it asks `isLineBreak` alongside `unicode.IsControl`, so the characters it neutralises are exactly the ones `Data`'s fold rewrites, plus the invisible FORMAT characters, which hide a spelling rather than end a line (#1663), plus the backtick, the double quote and «». The approximation it replaced was `r < 0x20 || r == 0x7f`, i.e. C0 and DEL, which left NEL (U+0085) and the Zl/Zp separators U+2028 and U+2029 raw — the last two are not control characters, so a project name holding one forged a line in the session-start block's own heading above the delimiter explainer, which is why the two classes are now asked of ONE shared set and why `memory.unprintableInIdentifier` refuses Zl/Zp on a project's id, name and path so the boundary and the renderer agree. `PreviewLine` is the ONE first-line cut of stored text: three copies had drifted, and a test per copy is what let them drift. `ShortID` is the ONE eight-character id form for an agent-facing surface (`mcpserver.shortID` is a delegation to it, and `cmd/ghost` and `memref.Short` keep their own because a report line is pasted back and an id there has to stay copyable): the eight are CHARACTERS measured by `memref.Short`, the result goes through `Token`, and a note is rendered bare between `(Note: ` and `)` with no `«…»` around it, so an id Token had to quote is shown whole rather than as eight runes of an escape. Every rune `isTokenRune` writes bare is ASCII, so the rune measurement is currently unobservable through `ShortID` and reverting it survives the suite — it is kept because it is the shared rule and because widening `isTokenRune` to a non-ASCII name is the one edit that makes it matter. `TagsLabel` is the ONE ` tags:[…]` label for the same reason `formatMemories` no longer marshals a tag list itself: two implementations of one rule are two rules, with the untested one shipping the bug. A tag list is a JSON array printed OUTSIDE `«…»`, and `json.Marshal` escapes a newline, a quote and a backslash but not `«»` or a backtick, so `neutralizeDelimiters` rewrites the delimiters as `<<`/`>>` — after the marshal, because substituting first introduces ASCII `<`/`>` that the encoder then HTML-escapes into noise — and a backtick becomes `\u0060`, the JSON escape the surrounding array already uses. Every rune of `Label`'s line-ending class (`isLineEndingOrControl`: the `isLineBreak` set plus C0, DEL and C1) and every invisible FORMAT character (`memory.IsFormatRune`: the whole of category Cf) is written as a JSON `\uXXXX` escape too, because `json.Marshal` leaves NEL (U+0085) and the rest of C1 raw and an imported tag is stored verbatim (#1488, #1663). The escape is a JSON one, so a rune outside the Basic Multilingual Plane is written as the SURROGATE PAIR JSON requires — JSON has no `\U` — and the array still decodes back to the stored tag. Two conventions differ on purpose: `<<` is what a content block prints for a literal `«`, so a reader who has met one knows the other, and there is no such convention for a backtick. A stored tag is neutralised rather than refused because refusing a value on the way OUT of a backup costs the user the whole memory — and the same field reaches `internal/reflection`'s consolidation prompt, which prints it with NEITHER JSON NOR delimiters on a line the model emits operations against, so a newline there ends the record. That surface escapes the field too, with `|` as its separator because a comma cannot be one, and it carries its own `neutralizeDelimiters` because that function is UNEXPORTED and assemble imports memory, so sharing it would mean exporting it. That surface's class is the LARGER one — every control character, not just the delimiters — and it also escapes its own separators, which is a semantic rather than a rendering problem: a tag holding the separator reads as a different tag COUNT, and `TestTheTwoTagSubstitutionsAgreeOnDelimiters` pins the spelling it shares with the row while `TestTheOrdinaryTagListIsUnchanged` pins that the escaping is invisible for every tag a real store holds. The ordinary cases stay byte-identical — `tags:["golden","pinned"]` is what every existing store, golden and test asserts on. `TestLabelKeepsOrdinaryTextAndNeutralisesTheRest`, `TestLabelNeverForgesALineWhateverItHolds`, `TestPreviewLineCutsAtACarriageReturnAsWellAsANewline`, `TestAGuillemetInsideATagCannotOpenADataBlock` and `TestShortIDMeasuresEightCharactersAndRendersThroughTheSafeToken` pin them. `TagsLabel` also BOUNDS each tag it prints at `MaxRenderedTagLen` with a `…[tag truncated]` marker, per tag rather than per list — see "A rendered label is bounded by the RENDERER" under Key patterns for the direction that makes it a bound and not a data change. Explain: `Request.Explain` projects THIS run into `Result.Explain` through `explainProjection` (`explain.go`) — one Request, one `Run`, included == `Result.Items`, every stored string through `Data`/`Token`, no retrieval record written (the record is the audit's count of answers delivered); the details are under the Hybrid search bullet. **EVERY MEMORY IS ONE PHYSICAL LINE; STORED LINE BREAKS ARE FOLDED TO `⏎` (#911).** `assemble.Data` — the one renderer for stored prose, and so for `Item.Line`'s content, `AgentLabel` and `SourceRefLabel` on every surface (search answers, `ghost_project_context`, the session-start block, the global resource, `ghost_memories_list`) — replaces LF, VT, FF, CR, FS/GS/RS, NEL (U+0085), LS (U+2028) and PS (U+2029) with `assemble.LineBreakEscape` (`⏎`, U+23CE), one escape per break and ONE for a CRLF pair, after the existing `«`/`»` neutralisation. The escape is a glyph rather than `\n` because a backslash escape is ambiguous with stored text that holds a backslash and an `n` and with the escapes `Token` and `Label` emit; `⏎` is not a delimiter, bracket, backtick, quote or whitespace and is not a line break to any reader. Every other control rune (C0, DEL and C1, so ESC and CSI included, plus the whole invisible FORMAT class — category Cf — the bidirectional controls U+200E, U+200F, U+202A–U+202E and U+2066–U+2069 among them, which reorder a line, and the zero-width ones U+200B, U+200C, U+200D, U+2060 and U+FEFF among the rest, which hide a spelling inside one; #1489, #1663) is printed as the visible ASCII escape `strconv.QuoteRuneToASCII` gives (`\x1b`, `\a`, `\x7f`, `\u009b`), the form `Label` uses, so no stored byte can act on a terminal; TAB alone is kept as written, since stored prose holds tabs and a tab starts no sequence. **EVERY INVISIBLE FORMAT CHARACTER IS ESCAPED, NOT ONLY THE BIDI CONTROLS (#1663).** `Data`, `Label` and `TagsLabel` ask ONE predicate for them — `memory.IsFormatRune`, the whole of Unicode category Cf, which lives in `internal/memory` beside `IsSafeTokenRune` because the renderers, the tag write boundary, the scope guard and the blank-text check all ask the same class — so a value that differs from another only in U+200B ZERO WIDTH SPACE, U+200C or U+200D (the non-joiner and the joiner), U+2060 WORD JOINER, U+FEFF ZERO WIDTH NO-BREAK SPACE or any other FORMAT character never prints as the other one: the class is invisible by definition, and an escape is the only way a reader can see it. A tag of `gh` + U+200B + `ost` printed as `ghost` before, which is a row that says one word and reads as another. The CATEGORY rather than a list of characters is the point: a list holds only what somebody thought of, and the bidi subset was exactly that (#1649, #1651). The stored value is never changed. The tag label writes the escape as JSON, so a rune outside the Basic Multilingual Plane is written as the surrogate pair JSON requires and the array decodes back to the stored tag. An escape is up to six bytes for one rune. The row budget is measured on the rendered `Item.Line()` (`outcome.go`), so it still holds; caps applied to RAW text before `Data` can now be exceeded by the render, as they already could by the three-byte line-break escape: the explain snippet (120 runes, `ExplainSnippet`), the session framing fields (`session_passive.go`) and the lifecycle marker error (`lifecycleMarkerErrorMaxBytes`, 300 bytes, a readability cap). The fold is one-way and display-only (the stored value is never changed); text with no line break or control rune is returned untouched, so goldens move only where stored text had one. The origin label is rendered through `assemble.SourceLabel` (`Token`), not printed verbatim, in `Item.Line` and in `mcpserver.formatMemories`. Pinned by `fold_test.go` in `internal/assemble`, `TestStoredLineBreaksNeverForgeAMemoryLine` (`internal/mcpserver`) and `TestSessionStartBlockNeverPrintsAForgedMemoryLine` (`internal/mcpinit`), which split on every line-break character, not only `\n`. `PreviewLine` cuts at the same set (`isLineBreak`). The per-package `quoteData` copies in `mcpserver`, `mcpinit` and `supersede` now delegate to `assemble.Data`; `reflection.quoteData` deliberately does not fold (its prompt output is stored, so a visible escape would be written back into merged memories), and `resolve.quoteData` does not fold itself: `renderNote` folds the stored content first and appends Ghost's own `\naudit:` evidence line after the closing delimiter, as structure for the classifier. `MaxBytes` and the agent and source_ref caps bound RAW, pre-fold text. **A near-duplicate loser the retriever REMOVES (`DropDemotedLosers`) is recorded, not lost (#894)**: it never enters `CandidateSet.Rows`, so the retriever reports it in `CandidateSet.DroppedLosers` (the row plus the sorted ids it lost to) and stage 6 files it as a dropped `dedup` / `near_duplicate` decision with `Decision.Against` naming the winners, in the trace, the stage's `DroppedIDs` and `droppedBy`. That one decision is the source for everything else: the retrieval record's dropped verdict, `CountsFor`'s `BucketTally.Deduped` (so the session-start header and the trace cannot disagree; `CountedAgainst` no longer infers losers from the store's eligible count, and a row nothing recorded is left uncounted rather than guessed a fate) and explain, which reports it as not included with `near_duplicate_of` set and a `near_duplicate_penalty` of 1 — the verdict that removed it, exactly as `DemotionPenalties` recorded it (the query path fills this itself since #926, and a row the ranking never stamped keeps the zero it is born with, identified by the pairing rather than by a number). Recording it changes no shown row, no ranking and no line. **Stage 7 (diversity) is a DEFERRAL and never a deletion (#927), on PASSIVE reads only**, and the five rules that make it one are the invariant, not the implementation. WHY PASSIVE ONLY: a query is a relevance question and the ranking is the whole answer to it, so moving the row that answered it behind another row because of what it is ABOUT is the stage answering a question nobody asked — while a digest asks no question and is handing a model everything worth knowing, where breadth is the point. The gate is `pipeline.passive`, bound from the ABSENCE of a `Query` and never from `Source`, because the consequences follow from the absence and a Source-keyed branch would let a future passive source be shared by a query-mode one. On a query-mode request the stage is a recorded no-op with no verdict of any kind, so `ghost_memory_search` and an explain on a search are byte-identical to a tree whose stage 7 was a pass-through; THE WINDOW IS PER BUCKET on a sliced read, and that is structural rather than cosmetic: stage 9 enforces each slice cap over the rows IT admits, so one shared window would let one bucket's share evict another bucket's row — which the per-bucket cap then readmits — leaving a `diversity_deferred` verdict on a row the answer RENDERS, counted as withheld by the bucket tally the session-start header reads — `record()` separately skips a drop decision for a row the answer carries, so the audit is the one reader that never files it. So the session start's project slice and `_global` slice are each divided by their own cap, each computed only over that bucket's rows, and a bucket with no item cap is not divided at all because the budget stage never cuts its rows on count. A bucket's rows keep their own relative order, so one bucket's reordering cannot change which rows stage 9 admits for another. The rules: within a window no category may take more than `ceil(window/2)` slots, minimum 1, where category is the memory's own field; a row past the cap is MOVED, in its existing relative order, to just after the window so the next-ranked rows of other categories take the slots, and if those run out the deferred rows come back in their original order until the window is full — so the stage can change WHICH rows fill a window and never how many; a PINNED row is never deferred, and it still counts toward its category's cap, because a pin is a slot guarantee (#936) and a deferral that took it back would be the guarantee in reverse; and nothing is reordered inside a category — inside a window or behind it, neither — because the retriever's order carries the keyword reservation, the status demotion, decay and both demotions and a second pass over it would undo them. The permutation keeps each half of the output (the rows that stayed inside a window, and the rows that moved behind it) in RANK order, which is what stops a pinned row admitted from behind a window from jumping ahead of an earlier-ranked row of its own category. A drop verdict is filed only for a row the stage MOVED, and `trim` WITHDRAWS one for any row it admits, so no answer shows a row the header calls withheld. The line between DEFERRED and NOT DEFERRED is drawn at the WINDOW and not at the share: a deferral moves a row to just after the window, which for a row already behind the window would move it UP, promoting exactly the rows the ranking decided against — so only a row the window was holding is deferred, one below it keeps its place and can still fill a vacated slot, and a store whose rows are all one category records no drop at all (every deferred row comes back) rather than re-describing rows the budget was already going to cut. Each deferred row is one decision at `stageDiversity` with reason `diversity_deferred` (with `diversity_backfilled` as the KEEP reason for one that came back, so a reader can tell a row that was never in question from one the stage moved and returned) and reads as not included wherever the verdict is projected (`explainReason`'s own sentence, unreachable from a shipped run because `Request.Explain` is refused without a query and the share is passive-only, kept so it cannot rot); `StageTrace.In == Out` at this stage because it removed nothing, and its `DroppedIDs` names the rows it moved behind the window rather than rows it removed, which is why the `In - Out = len(DroppedIDs)` identity the other stages hold is deliberately broken here. A row stage 7 left behind the window is then cut by stage 9, and it keeps stage 7's verdict: `trim` records a drop only for a row no stage has already judged, so one row is counted once in the per-stage breakdown, in `CountsFor`'s bucket tally and in the retrieval record — a stage that deferred a row and a stage that deleted one are different events and a count that had to satisfy an identity would have to lie about one of them. The rule is on the shared pipeline, so every PASSIVE surface that assembles inherits it and no surface has a second copy. Pinned rows a passive slice cap cut are counted in `Trace.PinnedCut` (stage 9 plus the retriever's `PinnedBeyond`) and `BucketTally.PinnedCut`; it is a subset of `RankedOut`, never added to `Total()`. Stage 8 (the relevance cutoff, [#954](https://github.com/wcatz/ghost/issues/954)) is stage 7's query-mode dual and is gated on the same request-bound `pipeline.passive` (a non-empty `Query`), so a query read records stage 7 as a pass-through and a passive read records the cutoff as one: one relative-to-top fused-Base cut (the score before the age decay), ONE parameter (`context.relevance_cutoff`, the share of the top row's Base), applied after dedup and before the budget, that can only SHORTEN a block — `limit` stays the maximum, the top row is always kept (result rate 1.000) and a pinned row and a keyword-reserved row are never cut. Each cut row is ONE decision at `stageCutoff` with reason `relevance_cutoff` (`Decision.Before` the fused score, no `Against`), read as a dropped verdict by `record()`, and its reason REACHES explain (the cutoff is a query rule and `Request.Explain` is refused without one, so unlike `diversity_deferred` its sentence is shipped and `explainReason` names the share). A passive request and an off cutoff (0 `context.relevance_cutoff`) are recorded pass-throughs with no note, so those blocks are byte-identical to a pipeline whose stage was absent. A PINNED row is never withheld by stage 5 (#975): it is kept whatever contradicts it, and `markConflicts` sets `Item.ContradictedBy` (`contradicted_by=`, bounded like `conflicts_with=`) from the same scope-filtered, de-duplicated adjacency, naming only partners newer than the pinned row that are withheld or still in the answer; `mcpinit`'s `sessionMemory` must carry the field. `Item.MaybeReplacedBy` is the retriever's lexical replacement guess (`Candidate.MaybeReplacedBy`) kept only for replacements still in the answer, set in ONE place (`markMaybeReplaced`, beside `markConflicts`/`markSuperseded` on every `fitResponse` pass, so a response-fit drop of the newer row clears it), on both modes, and rendered as `MaybeReplacedMarker` (`[maybe replaced by a newer note]`, in the prefix beside `[resolved]`, outside the «» data; its words alone prove nothing, because a tag is stored text in the same prefix and can hold them, JSON-quoted inside `tags:[…]`, so a check must find the marker at its field) plus a bounded `maybe_replaced_by=`; it marks and never withholds, and `sessionMemory` carries it too. `Item.SupersededBy` (`markSuperseded`, passive and query reads alike, pinned or unpinned rows, superseders on the page only; no stage reads the field, so it moves, demotes and withholds nothing and explain's `rank` stays the relevance rank: `TestRunQueryMarksASupersededRowWithItsSupersederOnThePage`, `TestRunQueryDoesNotMarkARowWhoseSupersederIsOffThePage`) renders `SupersededMarker` (`[superseded by a newer note]`) at that same prefix field, ahead of `MaybeReplacedMarker` when a row carries both, plus `superseded_by=`: the explicit relation switches the lexical guess off for its pair, so it must never render less than the guess did (see the `internal/memory/` bullet). **A REPLACEMENT MAY TAKE A SLOT, BUT ONLY AN UNRESERVED ONE.** `arrangeReplacements` (head of stage 9, passive reads only, per bucket) moves each named replacement to directly ahead of its older row. Inside the bucket's item cap the rows between shift down and every one stays inside it; from BEHIND the cap the replacement may displace exactly one row — the lowest-ranked in-cap row that is not pinned, not `FloorReserved`, not stage 7's (`diversityAdmitted`: a row the share brought in by other than rank) and not a replacement already placed — which then heads the rows behind the cap, so no other row crosses the cap. A plain shift would cut the cap's last row whatever had chosen it. With no such row nothing moves, the replacement stays off the page, and `markMaybeReplaced` names nothing, so the older line is unmarked rather than pointing at a row the reader cannot see. A pinned row is never displaced out of the cap and never moved ahead of or behind its pair (a pinned row BETWEEN an older row and its replacement shifts down by one and stays inside the cap); a row stage 7 deferred is never pulled back; a placed replacement is read as an older row next, so a chain comes out newest first. Pinned by `TestRunPassiveReplacementTakesOnlyAnUnreservedSlot` and `TestRunPassiveReplacementLeavesADiversityAdmittedRowItsSlot`. **ON A QUERY THE MOVE IS DISPLAY ORDER, MADE AFTER THE VERDICT.** `displayReplacements` runs ONCE, in `Run`, after `fitResponse` has returned, and is the only place a query answer is reordered. Everything a query decides is decided on the relevance order before it and is NOT recomputed after it: every stage's membership, the item caps, the response-fit trim (which therefore cuts the relevance-lowest row, not whichever row a move left last), the outcome and its reason, the abstention sentence, the machine line and the notes. The move is `arrangeBucket` over the whole page with no cap: the newer row of a pair whose rows are BOTH on the page goes to directly ahead of the older one and the rows between shift down by one. It adds no row and removes none, so the response is the same lines in another order and the same byte count; a replacement the caps or the trim cut is not pulled back and the older row is then unmarked; the pinned rule is the passive one (a pinned older row keeps its place, marked; a pinned replacement is not pulled). Only `Result.Items` and `Result.Response` follow the display order. Explain's `rank` stays the RELEVANCE rank (the place before the move) and the moved row says what moved through `moved_ahead_of` (set from `pipeline.movedAhead`, naming the row it is shown ahead of); the retrieval record's kept rows stay in relevance order, since the record is what retrieval decided. `Run` keeps the result as `fitResponse` left it and hands that copy to `explainProjection` and `emit` (`TestRunQueryExplainNamesTheRowAReplacementMovedAheadOf`, `TestRunQueryRecordKeepsTheRelevanceOrder`). Running it inside the fit loop or at stage 9 would let a byte-cap trim take a different row than relevance chose. Pinned by `TestRunQueryOrdersAValueReplacementAheadOfTheRowItReplaced`, `TestRunQueryMovesOnlyThePairedRow`, `TestRunQueryMoveNeverCrossesThePageBoundary`, `TestRunQueryByteCapCutsTheRelevanceLowestRow`, `TestRunQueryMoveLeavesTheVerdictAsRelevanceDecidedIt`, `TestRunQueryDoesNotMoveAPinnedPair` and `TestRunQueryDoesNotMoveRowsWhoseScopesDiffer`. `ghost_health` reports pinned rows with an open contradiction (`Store.PinnedRowsWithContradictions`) and nothing automatic acts on them. The no-answer step ([#955](https://github.com/wcatz/ghost/issues/955), `stageNoAnswer`, recorded as `no_answer`) runs directly after stage 8 and before the budget and is numbered with it, not as a new stage 9, so the numbered stages keep their numbers. It is QUERY-mode only (gated on the same request-bound `pipeline.passive`; a passive request records a silent pass-through with no block note, so a passive block stays byte-identical) and is ONE rule with ONE parameter (`Request.NoAnswerCosine`, `context.no_answer_cosine`): when the BEST `VectorScore` among the non-pinned rows reaching it is strictly below the bar, every non-pinned row is withheld with reason `nothing_cleared_the_bar` (a per-row decision, `p.dropped`, `droppedBy[no_answer]`, the retrieval record's dropped verdict and explain's excluded-row sentence like any other cut) and, when the block ends empty, `emptyReason`/`removalReason` short-circuit to that same reason ahead of `dominantRemoval` (the step withholds the whole block at once, so a larger removal count elsewhere must not outvote it) with an abstention sentence that states the best score and the bar. It never judges what it cannot compare: it passes through when the vector leg is not in play (no embedder, failed, or never applicable) or when no row carries a cosine, because `VectorScore` -1 is "the leg did not retrieve this row", not a low cosine. PRESENCE is the `ok` `rowCosine` returns and NEVER the sign, for the vector leg's own score and for the exact-cosine map alike: a cosine is in [-1, 1], so a genuinely negative one is a JUDGED (very weak) match that below the bar is withheld like any other, only the exact -1 mark means the leg did not carry the row, and a stored cosine is present because it is IN the map whatever its value. **THE SAME SENTINEL RULE DECIDES WHETHER AN ARM HELD A VALUE AT ALL** (`armValues`, which `verdict` consults before the floor): the vector arm counts as present whenever a row carries a `VectorScore` other than the exact -1, so a retriever that carries a cosine at or below zero gives the arm a value to judge and that block is `weak`/`below_floor` — the arm ran, held a value and judged the rows low — never `no_floor_arm`, the reason that says NEITHER arm had anything to compare. Reading a low score as absence told a caller no arm held a value, which sends them after an embedder for a verdict the vector leg had already made. It is a rule about the SEAM and Ghost's own leg is outside it: `*memory.Store` discards every candidate whose cosine is not above 0 before fusion (`minVectorSimilarity` in `internal/memory`), so a shipped query whose rows matched nothing on meaning still reports `no_floor_arm` and a `Candidate.VectorScore` there is either the sentinel or a positive cosine — the sentinel is the mark the rule is written against because it is the one the leg actually makes. The keyword arm's presence has always been the same test (`FTSRank >= 0` — rank 0 is a real first place and -1 is the mark a leg that retrieved nothing leaves), so the two arms test the same sentinel family rather than a score's sign. A pinned row is never withheld and is not judged; a keyword-reserved row is NOT exempt (the reservation puts the best keyword hits in every window, so an exemption would keep rows on exactly the near-miss queries the rule exists for). 0 is off and is byte-identical to a pipeline without the step. Nothing is added to the machine line. Only a row that carries a cosine is withheld: the step reads a row's own stored cosine (the optional `EmbeddingCosines` capability, loaded once in `Run` only when the bar is on) for rows the vector list did not carry, and a row with no cosine at all passes, so a partially embedded store never loses an exact keyword hit to the bar. The verdict field is set ONLY when the step itself left the block empty, so a block it passed through (it kept a row it could not judge) that a LATER stage then empties carries that stage's reason (`all_over_budget`) and never the "nothing cleared the bar" abstention sentence: the caller's next move differs, and lowering a bar that already passed the row is the wrong one. `config` ties the 0.62 default to `embedding.model` = `DefaultEmbeddingModel`: with another model and no explicit key (file or environment) the effective bar is 0 and `Request.NoAnswerBarNote` puts "no_answer bar off: unmeasured embedding model" in the stage's trace notes only (never in the block). **`SourceWorkingMoment` (`working_moment`, #973) is a block delivered beside a user message or an edit's result, and a surface that narrows a Result after `Run` records it through `RecordResult`, never through `Request.Record`:** `Run` records every successful call, so a caller that dedupes or floors afterwards would claim rows it never sent and write a record for every silent turn. The caller runs with `Record` nil, narrows `Items` to what it delivered, appends a dropped `Decision` per row it withheld, and calls `RecordResult` once and only on a delivery. `audit.KnownSources` carries the source so a report names it with `no rows` rather than hiding it. **The query-mode leg-consensus stage (#1251) admits a single-leg row only when its OWN leg ranked it FIRST** (vector-only rows need `VectorRank <= 0`, FTS-only rows need `FTSRank <= 0`; a row both legs found is always admitted). A row the vector leg could not see at all is admitted outright (#1319): `Candidate.Unembedded` is set by the retriever when the row carries no embedding the leg could score — no vector at all, one stamped with a different identity, or one of a width the query does not match — which is what a memory saved before the worker embedded it looks like, so its absent `VectorRank` is the leg's silence rather than its low rank and the consensus rule has nothing to judge it on. It is gated on the same request-bound `pipeline.passive` as stages 7 and 8, and on BOTH legs being live: a leg that is not applicable (no embedder) or ran and failed makes the stage a recorded pass-through, because with one leg live the consensus rule cannot apply and a rule that emptied the block would be answering an outage as if it were a verdict. The top-ranked row, a pinned row and a keyword-reserved row are always admitted, so the stage can never empty a query block and never undoes the reservation — the same three exemptions the cutoff and diversity stages apply. A STAND-IN for a removed near-duplicate (`DroppedLoser.StandIns`, see `internal/memory`) is admitted by this stage and by the relevance cutoff whenever the row it displaced would be (`standInEvidence`). Each row it withholds is ONE decision at `stageLegConsensus` with reason `leg_consensus`, read as a dropped verdict by `record()` and named by explain with the rule and both rank ceilings. **A QUERY ANSWER THE RESPONSE-FIT POST-PASS TRIMS DISCLOSES THE CUT IN ITS OWN TEXT (#1289).** `pipeline.responseCutNotice` appends the same notice the row-rendering listing tools render — built by the exported `assemble.CutNotice`, which the MCP `capResponseParts` renderer also calls, so the two renderers cannot spell one cut two ways — naming the count the machine line otherwise carried alone and the lever (`searchFitAdvice`) that reaches the rest. It is written INSIDE `pipeline.response`, which is the function the post-pass measures, so the notice is counted against `Budget.MaxBytes` like every other byte and a trim that earns one may cut one row more to pay for it; a notice the surface appended after the fit would ship bytes the cap never saw and put the answer over the budget it just cut to meet. It is rendered only for a non-passive answer that still holds a row: a PASSIVE block's surfaces render an abstention or their own bounded listing rather than this field, and an answer the cap EMPTIED already names the budget and the action through `reasonAllOverBudget`, so a notice there would describe rows the reader cannot see. `withCutNotice` in the assemble tests and the mcpserver assertions on a trimmed search's text prove both halves.

The `PreviewLine` function (used by `ghost_resolve_mark`, `ghost_resolve`, `ghost_link_withdraw`, `ghost_health` and the CLI listings) escapes C0/C1 controls and Unicode Cf format characters the same way `Data`, `Label` and `TagsLabel` do, and is bounded in its **rendered** form: an escape sequence counts as its full rune length, so the preview is at most the column width in runes plus the ellipsis when the text was cut.

- `internal/audit/` — retrieval audit (#646 part 2): heuristic comparison of what Ghost retrieved against what the agent WROTE, and the whole package is one line it refuses to cross. The transcript holds both what the agent said and what Ghost handed it, and the injected block repeats a memory's wording verbatim — so a comparison that read the whole file would report every retrieval as used, whatever the agent did, which is the one result that makes the audit worthless in the exact way it was built to detect. Only agent-authored regions count, and the three ingest paths are kept apart for three different rules: `AddProse` (assistant text) draws BOTH the token and the negation arms, `AddSaveArgs` (the arguments of Ghost's own save tools) is a restatement and gets its own arm, but an id in it IS a citation (#1010, `TestASaveThatNamesAMemoryCitesIt`), whether it stands in the save's text or its source_ref, and it gets the same negation rule as an id in prose (`TestANegatedIDInASaveIsADenialNotACitation`): "memory <id> is wrong, the real rule is ..." binds the id to the cue and is contradicted, never used, while the save's words never draw a negation arm; an id named by decision_record supersedes counted before this change and still does; an update with new content is still read as nothing; a save is a new memory's text, so an id in it names ANOTHER memory, the update-of-itself case that once justified ignoring it is already read as nothing before it arrives, and a save cannot name the row it creates; the words stay out of prose and the restatement bar is unchanged, because on the real-work set (3 positives, so any change is a hypothesis) no overlap rule separated the labelled restatements from the identical excerpts labelled NOT_USED or UNCLEAR beside them; a `ghost_memory_update` that carries new `content` is read as NOTHING by `hostevent.addToolCall` (decided on the arguments, so a tags-only or verified-only update, which carries no text, is still a citation by id) — its arguments are the memory's id and the text now stored, so any of the three routings files the memory against itself (used by wording, used by id, or superseded, which is decision-bearing and inverts the finding) — and is deliberately NOT in `ghostSaveToolNames`, which the save-nudge reads (`TestScanAuditAnUpdateOfAMemoryIsReadAsNothing`); the tools that act on a memory by id without its text (pin, promote, flag, delete, resolve_mark) stay citations, a deliberate reference, and `AddToolArgs` (every other tool call) gets NEITHER the negation arm nor a restatement — a tool argument is a string literal, so the cue words in it are the tool's, not a denial of anything, and routing them back through `AddProse` is what made `contradicted` fire on ordinary use (`TestAnOrdinaryUseIsNotAContradiction`). Precedence is contradiction > identifier > token > save restatement > ignored. **A CONTRADICTION IS A STRONG SIGNAL OR IT IS NOT ONE**, and both of its arms use the token arm's OWN bar (`clearsTokenBar`: at least 3 shared tokens AND at least a half of the memory's tokens, so `matched >= tokenFloor && matched*tokenFraction >= total`), because contradiction outranks every other verdict and a loose bar there reports ordinary use as a finding an operator would act on — a second, weaker threshold for negation was the original design and it was wrong. THE TOKEN ARM READS NEITHER A FILE NOR THE SESSION'S OWN VOCABULARY. A turn of `outlierTurnTokens` (150) or more distinct fingerprints is skipped by `matches` (a file write or a pasted document repeats half of almost any memory by size alone; real outlier turns had a median of 229 distinct tokens against 14), decided when the turn is JUDGED so a turn that grew across several Add calls is covered and a sidecar read back judges as the scan did, with nothing dropped from storage (`Empty`/`Ordered`/`Unplaced` still see it). A fingerprint in at least a quarter of a session's turns (over at least twelve; placed, non-outlier turns only) is generic (`genericFingerprints`), derived from the turn fingerprints the sidecar already carries, never stored, taken over the WHOLE session and carried onto each `Since` view, and it leaves the NUMERATOR of the token and save-restatement arms only (`TestGenericWordsDoNotMakeASupersede`: a false `superseded_in_session` is the decision-bearing one): the memory's total stays whole (shrinking it would shrink the bar), and the contradiction arms do not use it. The known cost is a real restatement that shares a turn with a giant write, which the bench's `fp` call reports as a miss. `tokenFraction` is SHARED: raising it to a half also makes the contradiction arms (`deniesByWording`) and the save-restatement arm need half of the memory's words, not a third, so a denial or a restatement that quotes only part of a memory is now missed; that recall cost is unmeasured on real work (the bench's labelled denials and save are all long quotations). The 150 line comes from the issue's measurement; the quarter and the twelve-turn minimum were chosen after a first draft's measurement and are pinned at their edges in `TestTheGenericLineIsPinnedAtItsEdges`, not derived from real data. The bar's fraction is a half and its UNIT is unchanged: `clearsTokenBar` is asked over ONE turn's fingerprints (`matches` loops the turns `SetAt` grouped the words into) and never over the session's union (#932), because a restatement is local — forty turns of domain vocabulary together clear a bar no single turn came near, and that was reported to an operator as a use. The bar is on the CUES being denials OF A CLAIM (`TestTheNegationCuesAreAllDenialsOfAClaim`), and the words must be the MEMORY'S OWN: the id arm needs a denial cue in the same SEGMENT as the id, so a cue in one sentence cannot satisfy an id named in another. **A CUE COUNTS ONLY AS A DENIAL OF THE MEMORY IT IS BOUND TO**, and every step of that is one rule rather than a matcher (#854). A cue is a run of WORDS matched on word boundaries (`negationCueWords` + `cueSpans` over `splitWords`), never a substring — "ignored"/"ignores" and "falsehood"/"falsely" are ordinary English, and an agent that named an id and then talked about its own ignored files had AGREED, which substring matching filed as the agent finding the memory wrong; every entry in the list is asserted in both directions by `TestACueIsMatchedAsWholeWords`, because a list matched as substrings passes "some cue works" for all fifteen.
